coupled to Maxwell, which reads

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{4\lambda}\int\differential[4]{x}\sqrt{-\gamma}\,\quantity{4-\sqrt{\gamma^{-1}g}\,\tensor{\gamma}{_{\mu\nu}}\tensor{g}{^{\nu\rho}}\tensor{\gamma}{_{\rho\sigma}}\tensor{g}{^{\sigma\mu}}}-\frac{1}{4}\int\differential[4]{x}\sqrt{-g}\,\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{g^{{{\nu\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\nu\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\nu\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\nu\rho}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.15826pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.95389pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.53847pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\rho}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\rho}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\rho}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.78293pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.3333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.80948pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\sigma\rho}}}}\mathchoice{g^{{{\sigma\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.75241pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10886pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.75241pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10886pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.75241pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10886pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\sigma\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.49626pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.75241pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.10886pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,.

(4.22)

This action has the form of (
2.1
), thus the deformed theory is given by integrating out the auxiliary metric. First, we verify that (
2.17
) is satisfied. Then, we can proceed by applying our formalism and one solution we find is the following
27
27

27

Here we should emphasise that if we were to consider the seed to be a non-abelian gauge theory, the solution for the auxiliary metric would differ from (
4.23
), since one must instead treat
\mathchoice{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{{a}}}_{{{\rho\sigma}}}}
as a single field with four Lorentz indices i.e.
\mathchoice{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}
. In this case, the resulting deformed action is also valid in the non-abelian limit. We should stress once more that the internal degrees of freedom do not enter at any point in this analysis, it is solely the index structure of
\mathchoice{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{X^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}}
that affects the behaviour of the deformation.

\displaystyle\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}=\mathchoice{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\rho}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\rho}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\rho}}}}{\gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.0113pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.48586pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.91846pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\rho}}}}\sqrt{\mathchoice{\delta^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}-\lambda\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{\rho\chi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.59926pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.99945pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\gamma^{{{\rho\chi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.59926pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.99945pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\gamma^{{{\rho\chi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.59926pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.99945pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\gamma^{{{\rho\chi}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.14552pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.59926pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.99945pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86581pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18985pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\chi\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86581pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18985pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\chi\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86581pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18985pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\chi\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.63048pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.86581pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.18985pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\chi\sigma}}}}\mathchoice{\gamma^{{{\sigma\tau}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.94197pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52997pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\gamma^{{{\sigma\tau}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.94197pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52997pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\gamma^{{{\sigma\tau}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.94197pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52997pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\gamma^{{{\sigma\tau}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.32181pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.94197pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.52997pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56256pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.25896pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56256pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.25896pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56256pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.25896pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.69714pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.56256pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.25896pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\tau\nu}}}}}\,,

(4.23)

therefore evaluating (
4.22
) on-shell yields the Born-Infeld action on a curved worldvolume

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int\differential[4]{x}\quantity{\sqrt{-\gamma}-\sqrt{-\text{det}(\tensor{\gamma}{_{\mu\nu}}+\sqrt{\la}\tensor{F}{_{\mu\nu}})}}\,.

(4.24)

At the same time, this model satisfies the flow equation (
1.1
) whose operator is given by
[
19
,
31
,
2
]

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}=\frac{1}{8}\quantity[\tr T^{2}-\frac{1}{2}(\tr T)^{2}]\,,

(4.25)

hence we aim to examine whether this operator satisfies (
2.4
) with the gravitational dressing given by (
4.22
). In this case the deformed stress-tensor is given by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\quantity(\tensor{\delta}{^{\mu}_{\nu}}-g\gamma^{-1}\tensor{g}{^{\mu\rho}}\tensor{\gamma}{_{\rho\sigma}}\tensor{g}{^{\sigma\tau}}\tensor{\gamma}{_{\tau\nu}})\,,

(4.26)

and the resulting flow equation, using matrix notation, yields

\displaystyle\text{det}(g^{-1}\gamma)=\frac{1}{8}(\text{tr}(g^{-1}\gamma)^{2})^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\text{tr}(g^{-1}\gamma)^{4}\,.

(4.27)

Obviously, this relation does not hold for arbitrary auxiliary metric
\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}
and even in its current form, it is a highly non-trivial task to check whether (
4.27
) holds on-shell for (
4.23
). To make the problem tractable, we need to remove the square root dependence, which can be achieved by expressing (
4.27
) exclusively in terms of the “squared” matrix
(\gamma^{-1}g)^{2}
. After some algebra, we collect the exact
\lambda
dependence order-by-order and require each term to vanish. When the dust settles, this yields the following two conditions

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}(\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{2})^{4}-4\,(\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{2})^{2}\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{4}-4\,(\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{4})^{2}+16\,\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{8}=0\,,\\
(\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{2})^{3}-6\,\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{2}\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{4}+8\,\text{tr}(\gamma^{-1}F)^{6}=0\,,\end{gathered}

which we verified hold for arbitrary field strength and background metric. Hence, we find that the flow equation (
2.4
) with the operator given by (
4.25
) is valid only on-shell for the auxiliary metric when the seed theory is Maxwell, as anticipated from our discussion in Section
2
. This can mean one of two things; either the mass term of (
4.22
) or the operator is not universal. In the former case, one should in principle look for solutions to the flow equation for this operator, which satisfy the usual initial conditions (
2.17
). Unfortunately, we could not find any as the resulting equation is a highly non-linear PDE, we therefore proceed by focusing on the latter case.

In other words, we now aim to investigate whether we can construct a universal operator corresponding to the case of arbitrary
S_{0}
coupled to the dressing of (
4.22
).
To this end, we should rewrite (
4.22
) in terms of the
Y_{i}
matrices as in (
2.1
). After some massaging and using the properties of the elementary symmetric polynomials
\det_{\kappa}
defined in Appendix
A
we can rewrite the gravitational dressing, using matrix notation, as follows

\displaystyle S_{G}=\frac{1}{4\lambda}\int\differential[4]{x}\sqrt{-\gamma}\,\quantity{4-\frac{\text{det}_{3}Y_{1}^{4}}{\text{det} Y_{1}^{3}}}\,.

(4.30)

Following the massive gravity approach and by fitting an order four polynomial in terms of power-traces of the stress-tensor, we find that the corresponding operator is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcall{O}=\frac{16b_{0}-1}{4\lambda}\tr T+2b_{0}\quantity[\tr T^{2}-(\tr T)^{2}]+\frac{2\lambda b_{0}}{3}\quantity[(\tr T)^{3}-3\tr T \tr T^{2}+2\tr T^{3}]&\\
-\frac{\lambda^{2}b_{0}}{6}\quantity[(\tr T)^{4}-6(\tr T)^{2}\tr T^{2}+3(\tr T^{2})^{2}+8\tr T \tr T^{3}-6\tr T^{4}]&\,,\end{aligned}

(4.31)

where
b_{0}
is arbitrary.
This implies that the terms proportional to
b_{0}
cancel, which yields

\displaystyle\tr T=\lambda\text{det}_{2}T-\lambda^{2}\text{det}_{3}T+\lambda^{3}\text{det}T\,.

(4.32)

Let us now compare this trace-flow equation with (
3.39
). First, we notice that there are no curvature nor equation of motion terms. This implies that the seed action
S_{0}
is necessarily invariant under the Weyl transformations (
3.37
) therefore, the gravitational dressing (
4.30
) can only be used to deform theories of this type. By taking this into account together with the fact that the scaling dimension of
\lambda
is given by
\Delta_{\lambda}=-4
, we find that the true operator reads

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{4}=-\frac{1}{4}\quantity[\text{det}_{2}T-\la\text{det}_{3}T+\la^{2}\text{det}T]\,.

(4.33)

We can now proceed with a few comments. First, we verify that for the Born-Infeld action (
4.24
), the operator at hand reduces to (
4.25
), however we find that the Dirac-Born-Infeld action is not part of this flow. Second, we can compare the structure of (
4.33
) with its three-dimensional analogue coming from the D2-brane flow equation, given by
[
71
,
9
]

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{3}=-\frac{1}{2}\quantity[\text{det}_{2}T-\la\text{det}T]\,,

(4.34)

and so we observe that a potential
d
-dimensional operator has the following structure

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{d}=\frac{1}{2^{d-2}\lambda^{2}}\quantity[1-\la\tr T-\text{det}(\mathds{1}_{d}-\la\,T)]\,.

(4.35)

To this end, it would be interesting to investigate the exact form of the corresponding deformed action for
d\geq 5
and construct its massive gravity description. Finally, to our surprise, the operator (
4.35
) is exactly of the same form as (
4.3
) under the following transformation for the stress-tensor and simple rescaling of
\lambda

\displaystyle\lambda\longrightarrow 2^{1-d/2}\lambda\,,\qquad T\longrightarrow-2^{d/2-1}\quantity(T-\frac{\tr T }{d-1}\mathds{1}_{d})\,.

(4.36)

This could hint at a universal underlying structure that governs these types of deformations.

4.4
Spin zero, and a comment on deformed actions

In a slightly different spirit, we would like to explore other limits of the Dirac-Born-Infeld action so for this subsection, we will turn off the gauge fields i.e. we will be considering the following deformed theory

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int\differential[d]{x}\quantity{\sqrt{-\gamma}-\sqrt{-\det(\tensor{\gamma}{_\mu_\nu}+\la\tensor{\Phi}{_\mu_\nu})}}\,,

(4.37)

where
\mathchoice{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\Phi^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
is given by (
3.16
). The operator that appears in the corresponding flow equation was found to be
[
9
]

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{d}=\dfrac{1}{2\lambda^{2}}\quantity[d-2-\la\tr T-(d-2)\qty[\det(\mathds{1}_{d}-\la T)]^{\frac{1}{d-2}}],

(4.38)

where we once again notice that a trace/determinant structure is present. Interestingly, there does exist another classical operator when one considers the single boson case
[
30
]

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}^{\prime}_{d}=\dfrac{1}{2d}\text{tr}(T^{2})-\dfrac{1}{d^{2}}\tr(T)^{2}-\dfrac{d-2}{2\sqrt{d-1}d^{3/2}}\tr(T)\sqrt{\text{tr}(T^{2})-\dfrac{1}{d}\tr(T)^{2}}\,,

(4.39)

which only coincides with (
4.38
) in that limit. To make matters worse, we managed to derive a third equivalent operator for this case reading

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcall{O}^{\prime\prime}_{d}=\frac{1}{2d}\lambda\text{tr}(T^{3})-&\frac{1}{2(d-1)}\lambda\text{tr}(T^{2})\tr(T)+\frac{1}{2d(d-1)}\lambda\tr(T)^{3}\\
&+\frac{d(d-3)+3}{2d(d-1)}\text{tr}(T^{2})-\dfrac{1}{2(d-1)}\tr(T)^{2}\,,\end{aligned}

(4.40)

which enjoys a nicer polynomial form compared to other two.
28
28

28

We should note here that we were not able to derive the massive gravity description for all three operators.
In order to make our point transparent, let us restate that we have established the following classical equality for the single boson case

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{d}=\mathcall{O}^{\prime}_{d}=\mathcall{O}^{\prime\prime}_{d}\,,

(4.41)

therefore, is it fair to ask which operator would be the one to consider in the end, especially if we want to include quantum effects. The logical choice would be
\mathcal{O}_{d}
since this operator is valid also for the sigma model, however this is still not the most general case since we have no gauge field. This means that we might once again be in a position where the current structure of the deformed action allows for other equivalent operators, bringing us in the same awkward spot but a “level” above. In other words, it is not safe to extrapolate by considering a specific example and then claiming the resulting operator as universal, unless one is absolutely sure that the deformed theory at hand contains all the information about the resulting deformation. One can also notice this for the case of the Born-Infeld action of the previous subsection, the naive operator there is (
4.25
) and we found the more (but not most) general operator to be (
4.33
). If we assume that a single D
p
-brane is the correct starting point then only the corresponding operator of this model should be considered as a candidate for the higher-dimensional analogue. This of course does not mean that the operators derived by specific limiting cases are not interesting; they can be used as the first check against generalized operators in these limits, exactly like the analysis above.

Alternatively, one can completely avoid all the aforementioned issues by letting go of the specific deformed theory and attack the problem using our method, which by construction generates deformations that are universal and remains agnostic about the nature of
S_{0}
. There, we still need to make assumptions regarding the structure of the gravitational mass term and deformation,
29
29

29

Which should however obey (
2.4
) and (
2.17
).
as the connection between stress-tensor deformations and massive gravity is still not well-understood.

4.5
A potential for
(\tr T)^{n}

In this final subsection we focus on constructing the necessary toolkit for tackling the problem of finding solutions to the flow equation for theories that are deformed via a polynomial of traces of the stress-tensor, in
d
dimensions. To gain intuition, we start from the two-dimensional case, propose its
d
-dimensional generalization and finally apply our results to a four-dimensional example, extending at the same time already known findings. This being said, let us begin by considering the flow equation of a theory living in two dimensions. Specifically, we aim to find solutions for the following operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}=\int\differential[2]{x}f\quantity{b_{1}(\tr T)^{2}+b_{2}\dfrac{\tr T}{\lambda}+b_{3}\dfrac{1}{\lambda^{2}}}\,,

(4.42)

where
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})
are arbitrary dimensionless constants. Applying our formalism, but not (
2.17
), we reach the following solution,

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=-\dfrac{1}{16b_{1}\lambda}\int\differential[2]{x}f\{4b_{2}+(\mathchoice{f^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{f^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{f^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}{f^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}}\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$ (4.47)

therefore both approaches are consistent with each other. From here we observe that all the bosonic degrees of freedom completely decouple since they “belong” to the traceless part of the stress-tensor of the seed theory. We may now use this result to retrieve the following deformed action by plugging it in (
4.43
) leading to

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=\int\differential[2]{x}f\quantity{-\dfrac{b_{2}}{4\la b_{1}}-\dfrac{1}{2}\tensor{\gamma}{^{\mu}^{\nu}}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}+\dfrac{\la b_{1}(\tensor{f}{^{\mu}_{a}}\tensor{\Psi}{^{a}_{\mu}})^{2}-2\tensor{f}{^{\mu}_{a}}\tensor{\Psi}{^{a}_{\mu}}-4V}{4(1+4V\lambda b_{1})}}\,,

(4.48)

which up to trivial redefinitions agrees with the deformed action found in
[
56
]
when the fermions are turned off. As a consistency check we note that (
4.48
) obeys (
4.42
) when (
4.44
) is valid.

Equipped with this example in two dimensions, we can now proceed to its higher-dimensional analogue.
We begin from the most general case of the family of trace deformations, that is

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}=\int\differential[d]{x}f\,\mathcall{O}(\lambda,\tr T)\,,

(4.49)

for which, it is natural to assume that the deformed action will take the following form

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=S_{G}+S_{0}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int\differential[d]{x}f\hskip 1.42271ptK(y_{1})+S_{0}\,.

(4.50)

Then, the flow equation for this dressing (
2.4
) yields the higher-dimensional equivalent of (
3.58
) that is

\displaystyle K(y_{1})=-\lambda^{2}\mathcall{O}(\lambda,[d\,K(y_{1})-y_{1}K(y_{1})^{\prime}]/\lambda)\,,\qquad K(y_{1})^{\prime}\coloneqq\derivative{K(y_{1})}{y_{1}}\,,

(4.51)

which is subject to the usual initial conditions (
2.17
). Crucially, the solutions of the equation above provide a complete description for this family of deformations. Moreover notice that we have managed to reduce the problem to a non-linear ODE instead of a PDE, however it is still very complicated even for simple examples. To give a few, first, let us examine the quadratic case to make contact with (
4.42
) and apply it to a four-dimensional model and then, continue with the scenario where we only pick one term that is
\sim(\tr T)^{n}
.
31
31

31

We were able to tame (
4.51
) only for these two cases.
For the quadratic case we find the following solution

\displaystyle K(y_{1})=-\dfrac{1}{2d^{2}b_{1}}\quantity[2\qty(\qty[\frac{y_{1}}{d}]^{d/2}-1)^{2}+db_{2}]\,,\qquad b_{3}=\dfrac{b_{2}(2+db_{2})}{4db_{1}}\,.

(4.52)

At
d=2
we remark that we recover the gravitational dressing of (
4.43
), as required/expected, and if (
2.17
) is applied, once again

in any

one retrieves the kernel of (
3.54
) for
b_{1}=-1
.

Given this solution let us further check its validity with a concrete example in four dimensions. Specifically, let us consider a sigma model of spin-0 and spin-1 bosons interacting with an arbitrary potential, that is

For

\displaystyle S_{0}=\int\differential[4]{x}e\,\quantity{-\dfrac{1}{4}\tensor{F}{_{\mu\nu}}\tensor{g}{^{\mu\rho}}\tensor{F}{_{\rho\la}}\tensor{g}{^{\la\nu}}-\dfrac{1}{2}\tensor{g}{^{\mu}^{\nu}}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}-V}\,.

(4.53)

) with the necessary “corrections” to the traceless part of the stress-tensor of (

). Then the equations of motion for the complete action take the following form

\displaystyle\dfrac{\delta S_{\lambda}}{\delta\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}-\quantity(\dfrac{\delta S_{0}}{\delta\tensor{e}{_{\m}^{a}}}-\dfrac{\tensor{e}{^{\m}_{a}}}{4} \tensor{e}{_{\n}^{b}}\dfrac{\delta S_{0}}{\delta\tensor{e}{_{\n}^{b}}})=0\,,

b_{1}}{1+16V\lambda b_{1}}}\mathchoice{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{f^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(4.55)

which is a
\lambda
-dependent Weyl transformation, verifying (
4.45
) and the arguments surrounding it. Notice that the spin-1 fields do not contribute to the solution, as the corresponding stress-tensor is traceless in four dimensions, in direct analogy with the bosons in two dimensions. Then, plugging this solution in (
4.50
) we reach

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=\int\differential[4]{x}f\quantity{-\frac{b_{2}}{8\lambda b_{1}}-\dfrac{1}{4}\tensor{F}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}\tensor{\g}{^{\mu}^{\rho}}\tensor{F}{_{\rho}_{\la}}\tensor{\g}{^{\la}^{\nu}}-\frac{\tensor{\g}{^{\mu}^{\nu}}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}}-2\lambda b_{1}(\tensor{\g}{^{\mu}^{\nu}}\tensor{\Phi}{_{\mu}_{\nu}})^{2}+2V}{2(1+16V\lambda b_{1})}}\,,

(4.56)

which, up to appropriate rescalings, agrees with the deformed action previously derived in
[
56
]
. Moreover, we notice that the above deformed action satisfies the initial flow equation (
4.42
) we aimed to solve, when
b_{3}
is given by (
4.52
). Another observation we can make by comparing (
4.48
) and (
4.56
) is that spin-0 and spin-1/2 fields in
d=2
behave in the same way as spin-1 and spin-0 fields do in
d=4
.
Finally, looking back at (
4.46
) and (
4.54
), it is clear that the equations of motion in arbitrary dimensions now take the following form

\displaystyle\dfrac{\delta S_{\lambda}}{\delta\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}-\quantity(\dfrac{\delta S_{0}}{\delta\tensor{e}{_{\m}^{a}}}-\dfrac{\tensor{e}{^{\m}_{a}}}{d} \tensor{e}{_{\n}^{b}}\dfrac{\delta S_{0}}{\delta\tensor{e}{_{\n}^{b}}})=0\,.

(4.57)

Combining these relations with (
4.51
) allows one to fully determine the solution for the auxiliary vielbein.

Finally, we are now in a position where we could tackle a more complicated operator. As it was mentioned before, consider now the following deformation

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}=\int\differential[d]{x}f\lambda^{n-2}b_{1}(\tr T)^{n}\,,

(4.58)

where
b_{1}
is an arbitrary dimensionless constant and
n>1
. For this choice (
4.51
) becomes

\displaystyle K(y_{1})=-b_{1}(d\,K(y_{1})-y_{1}K(y_{1})^{\prime})^{n}\,,

(4.59)

therefore given (
2.17
), the solution reads

\displaystyle K(y_{1})=-\quantity[\dfrac{\qty(y_{1}/d)^{d(n-1)/n}-1}{db_{1}^{1/n}}]^{n/(n-1)}\,.

(4.60)

Similarly as before, combining the above with (
4.57
) we have everything we need to determine the solution to the auxiliary vielbein and obtain a deformed action. One can now easily verify that for
n=2
we obtain (
4.52
) with
b_{2}=0
, but also for
d=2
and (
3.15
) without fermions the resulting theory matches precisely
[
62
]
in the single boson limit. In any case, we must note however, that the gravitational structure at hand is highly non-linear and does not resemble or share any properties with dRGT gravity.

5  Summary and discussion

In this paper we showed how the massive gravity formulation of stress-tensor deformations in arbitrary dimensions can lead to universal results as well as techniques, which can be used to tackle different problems. To demonstrate the power of this approach, we started exploring two dimensions and used the tool of perturbation theory. As a first consistency check, we show how one can recover
T\overline{T}
enhanced with
\tr T+\Lambda_{2}
, proving that this extension is not unique to the massive gravity approach but holds in general. In addition, by studying the deformed action of two different models that feature no fermions but are characterized by a non-trivial potential, we perform a perturbative expansion in a suitably chosen scalar parameter, to find an infinite extension of Schwarz’s list
[
64
]
. More concretely, for the first case we retrieve (
3.23
) in accordance with
[
17
,
11
]
and in the second we find that the following Taylor expansion holds

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{3^{n-5/2}(-y)^{n}}{\Gamma(1+n)}{}_{5}\widetilde{F}_{4}\biggl[\genfrac{.}{.}{0.0pt}{}{\frac{1}{2},\,\,\frac{3}{4},\,\,1,\,\,1,\,\,\frac{5}{4}}{2,\frac{3-n}{3},\frac{4-n}{3},\frac{5-n}{3}};\tfrac{64}{27}z\biggr]=\frac{(1-y)^{2}}{4\pi\mathcal{H}^{2}}-\frac{(\mathcal{H}-1)^{2}}{\pi z(1-y)}\,,

(5.1)

where we defined
\mathcal{H}\coloneqq H_{1,+}
, which is given by (
3.26
) and (
3.2
). A natural next step would be to examine whether other deformed actions also yield similar insights, we hope to report on this in the near future. Crucially, while our methods are anchored on the massive gravity approach, a by-product of our analysis is the formulation of an inverse algorithm, initiating the study of the algebraic properties of special functions directly through the
T\overline{T}
programme. In particular, we argued that this approach can be potentially applied to other special functions, and given a suitable scalar parameter to perform the perturbative expansion in, it determines whether the function admits an underlying algebraic structure or not.

Switching gears, we clarified the role of the trace-flow equation,
33
33

33

We achieve this in any dimension.
which is secretly a consequence of the local RG flow
[
60
]
, and highlighted the importance of Weyl symmetry for the underlying theory. We concluded our low-dimensional analysis performing multiple checks in one dimension, achieving among other things a precise match with the gravitational dressing proposed in
[
41
]
. Notably, an essential tool in establishing this were the universal initial conditions for stress-tensor deformations in arbitrary dimensions (
2.17
), that we derived in Section
2
using the massive-gravity approach. Furthermore, we verified that these initial conditions hold in all examples that were analysed in this work, thereby confirming their robustness.

Next, we analysed the additional terms appearing in our higher-dimensional analogue of the
T\overline{T}

operator. Specifically, starting from the general mass term in
d
dimensions (
4.1
), applying (
2.17
) and combining it with the flow equation given by (
2.4
), we find that the resulting operator is given by

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{d}=\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\quantity[1+\la\tr\mathcal{T}-\det(\mathds{1}_{d}+\la\mathcal{T})]\,,\qquad\mathcal{T}\coloneqq T-\frac{\tr T}{d-1}\mathds{1}_{d}\,.

(5.2)

Subsequently, by expressing this relation in terms of power-traces of the stress-tensor, we find that it can be written as an order
d
polynomial in
\lambda
, that is

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{d}=\frac{1}{2}\quantity[\tr T^{2}-\frac{1}{d-1}(\tr T)^{2}]+\text{finite $\lambda$ corrections}\,.

(5.3)

Comparing this with the earlier holographic studies of
[
23
,
5
,
68
,
45
]
we find that the zeroth order term exactly reproduces the
T^{2}
operator. Moreover, the fact that the large
N
limit yields precisely this latter operator, implies that one can interpret (
5.3
) as an expansion in
1/N
, suggesting that the additional terms initially found in
[
71
]
may capture all
1/N
corrections. From the massive gravity perspective, when implementing the symmetric vielbein condition as our gauge choice, we find that the corresponding mass term takes the following form

\displaystyle S_{G}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int\differential[d]{x}\sqrt{-\gamma}\,\quantity{d-1-\tr\sqrt{\g^{-1}g}+\det\sqrt{\g^{-1}g}}\,,

(5.4)

which is remarkably the higher-dimensional version of the famous four-dimensional ghost-free minimal massive gravity action
[
46
,
47
,
24
,
25
]
. From there we found that the leading order
T^{2}
operator of (
5.3
) is directly related to the linearized Fierz-Pauli dressing, recovering at the same time the Hubbard-Stratonovich interpretation of
[
14
,
45
]
.
This implies that all subleading terms essentially entail its non-linear completion, and as a result ensures the absence of spin-2 ghosts. Said differently, we show that the existence of the higher order terms is in this sense imperative for the consistent formulation of a gravitational theory of this type. We should however mention that we do not yet have a clear interpretation of these terms from a field theory point of view. It would be most interesting to further investigate this.

Furthermore, we studied the flat space limit of this gravitational dressing with the hope of uncovering a dynamical coordinates perspective. Surprisingly, we were able to derive a deformed action that lacks gravitational elements and is solely expressed in terms of the undeformed field theory data
{\mathcal{L}}_{0}
and
T_{0}
. Explicitly, we have

\displaystyle S_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{\lambda}\int\differential[d]{\mathcal{Y}}\,\quantity{d-1-\qty[d-1-\la\qty(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\tr T_{0})]\det(\mathds{1}_{d}+\la T_{0})^{\frac{1}{1-d}}}\,,

(5.5)

accompanied with the following field-dependent change of coordinates

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mathcal{Y}^{\nu}}{x^{\mu}}=\dfrac{(\det[\mathds{1}_{d}+\lambda T_{0}])^{\frac{2-d}{d-1}}}{(d-1)!}\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\mu_{2}}{\cdots}{\mu_{d}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\mu_{2}}{\cdots}{\mu_{d}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.25pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.67499 {0}}^{{{\mu_{2}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.95927pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.95927pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.67052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.97893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{2}}}}}{{T_{0}}^{{{\mu_{2}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.95927pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.95927pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.67052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.97893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{2}}}}}{{T_{0}}^{{{\mu_{2}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.95927pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.95927pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.67052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.97893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.81232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.20248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.35892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{2}}}}})\cdots(\mathchoice{\delta^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}}+\lambda\mathchoice{{T_{0}}^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}}{{T_{0}}^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}}{{T_{0}}^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}}{{T_{0}}^{{{\mu_{d}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.05966pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.74222pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.05063pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.9127pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.27419pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.43062pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu_{d}}}}})\,.

(5.6)

Staring at these for while, one realizes that this is a
d
-dimensional counterpart of the two-dimensional dynamical coordinates formulation of
T\overline{T}

[
20
]
. To further substantiate this, we also show that the saddles of the deformed and undeformed theories match (
2.10
), suggesting that the equation above serves as the map between the two. As mentioned previously, verifying that this coordinate transformation indeed gives the correct deformed action, proves to be a highly non-trivial task. However, we have checked our results by limiting ourselves to the case where the seed theory only contains a potential and no kinetic terms. Nonetheless, we aim to further test our findings with more complicated examples and report on this in future work.

Focussing for a moment on
d=4
and the Born-Infeld action, we studied the curved background version of the gravitational dressing first introduced in
[
34
]
, coupled to Maxwell theory. As a result, we find an operator that can be used to deform any Weyl invariant seed theory in four dimensions. By comparing this deformation to the one corresponding the D2-brane action, we observe a common structure and propose that its
d
-dimensional generalization reads

\displaystyle\mathcall{O}_{d}=\frac{1}{2^{d-2}\lambda^{2}}\quantity[1-\la\tr T-\text{det}(\mathds{1}_{d}-\la\,T)]\,.

(5.7)

From there we observe that (
5.2
) and (
5.7
) are related by an appropriate transformation (
4.36
) of the stress-tensor
T
and the deformation parameter
\lambda
. Given this connection between these seemingly distinct operators it would be interesting to understand whether there also exists a similar equivalence between their gravitational counterparts.

Continuing our
d
-dimensional journey, we comment on the different operators that can be obtained by investigating the flow equation when the deformed theory is given by the Dirac-Born-Infeld action in curved spacetime with the gauge fields turned off. Presently, we are unable to reconstruct the massive gravity description of these different operators, but it would nonetheless be interesting to achieve this. We expect that this requires implementing a suitable ansatz for the dressing in terms of the
Y_{i}
matrices that differs from the ones studied in this work.

Finally, we studied a certain family of stress-tensor deformations in
d
dimensions, whose operators are exclusively expressed as a polynomial of
\tr T
. Gaining intuition from specific seed actions, we find that such deformations have no effect when we consider Weyl invariant seed theories. Consequently, our analysis suggests that the background is also invariant under Weyl, hence the equations of motion of the auxiliary vielbein
\mathchoice{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{e^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{a}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
must be supplemented with appropriate “correction” terms (
4.57
). In addition, we derive the corresponding gravitational dressing for certain limiting cases of the respective operator, which interestingly do not resemble any known mass term. Equipped with these results, we now have the necessary toolkit to tackle the problem of finding a deformed action for any operator of this family.

Apart from the open problems discussed above, it would interesting to further understand how one would proceed to deform a purely gravitational theory or more generally, a seed action that contains a dynamical background. Some work has already been done in two
[
11
]
and four
[
56
]
dimensions, with the motivation coming from dilaton and
f(R)
gravities respectively. We have deliberately postponed this discussion to this section since it is clear from our point of view that integrating out an auxiliary vielbein for some stress-tensor deformation in this case, can be equivalent to a full-blown GR problem i.e. the solutions (if they exist) are to be given by a set of non-linear PDE’s. That is, if one is working in the second order formalism where the spin connection is usually picked to be the torsion-less Levi-Civita, which is the case for
[
11
]
. There, the authors were only able to find a leading order approximation of the deformed theory containing non-trivial higher-derivative terms of the matter fields. We can now identify the source of this complexity with the non-linear and differential nature of the resulting equations of motion for the auxiliary background.

On the other hand, one can postpone this issue if the first order formalism is employed instead
[
56
]
. The spin connection can now be treated as an independent quantity and as a result, any
f(R)
kinetic term will only contain an algebraic dependence on the background. This automatically means that finding the saddle point for the background now reduces to a solution of an algebraic equation, which is in principle, solvable. Assuming that this can be done, it is then understood that the deformed action will contain an undetermined spin connection that one needs to integrate out. We can then identify two interesting and potentially problematic issues that may arise. The first would be that it is not really clear if these two steps necessarily commute, considering that this type of situation is identical to what happens with fermions on a curved background, see
[
35
]
for a very nice explanation. The second and more serious concern is that, at the end of the day, one still needs to integrate out the spin connection in order to reach the physical theory, which may not be feasible considering the intricate structure at hand. Nevertheless, we are very curious to find out if there is a concrete way of including dynamical gravity in the mix, as the current understanding of these deformations seems to be restricted to ordinary matter.

Appendix A
The
\kappa
-determinant

Here we define the
\kappa
-determinant as the elementary symmetric polynomials of a given
d\times d
matrix
A

[
49
]

\displaystyle\text{det}_{\kappa}(A)\coloneqq\frac{(-1)^{\kappa}}{\kappa!}B_{k}(-\tr A,-1!\tr A^{2},-2!\tr A^{3},\dots,-(\kappa-1)!\tr A^{\kappa})\,,

(A.1)

where
B_{\kappa}
is the complete exponential Bell polynomial. Intuitively, one may think about this expression as a generalization of the determinant of
A
, but by treating the matrix as if it was
\kappa\times\kappa
instead of
d\times d
-dimensional. Hence, it satisfies the following properties

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{det}_{\kappa}(\lambda A)=\lambda^{\kappa}\text{det}_{\kappa}(A)\,,\qquad\text{det}_{\kappa}(\mathds{1}_{d})=\binom{d}{\kappa}\,,\qquad\text{det}_{\kappa}(A^{\text{T}})&=\text{det}_{\kappa}(A)\,,\\
\text{det}_{0}(A)\coloneqq 1\,,\qquad\text{det}_{1}(A)\coloneqq\tr(A)\,,\qquad\text{det}_{d}(A)&\coloneqq\det(A)\,,\end{aligned}

(A.2)

as well as

\displaystyle\text{det}_{\kappa}(A^{-1})=\frac{\text{det}_{d-\kappa}(A)}{\det( A)}\,,

(A.3)

where
\lambda
is a constant.
